    11	
    12	The ACP permission boundary that lets Atlas read tools continue automatically but pauses every Atlas write tool on a typed change review. It offers allow_once or reject_once and deliberately hides allow_always for semantic writes.   120	**Status**: active.
   121	
   122	## 2026-08-24 (113) — The write checkpoint moves into the server that performs the write
   123	
   124	**Prior decisions**: (111) removed `codex-acp` from in-app chat because a Codex
   125	`read-only` session let an Atlas MCP write reach disk with no permission request,
   126	and named the way back: *"an app-owned MCP proxy or server capability token
   127	reliably pauses every Codex Atlas write."* This record builds that checkpoint. It
   128	does **not** restore Codex chat — (111) stays active until its installed-app
   129	proof exists.
   130	
   131	**Observed cause**: Codex's approval policy documents its own scope — it decides
   132	"when the model requires human approval before executing **a command**". MCP tool
   133	calls were never inside it. Reading `codex-acp` confirms the shape: the adapter
   134	builds permission requests from `item/commandExecution/requestApproval` and
   135	`item/fileChange/requestApproval`, and there is no MCP-tool equivalent. So the
   136	gate the screen promised did not exist on the wire, and no third-party
   137	configuration could have supplied it.
   138	
   139	**The lever we already own**: the same adapter forwards
   140	`mcpServer/elicitation/request` into ACP `session/request_permission`, and the
   141	MCP SDK this server runs on (`@modelcontextprotocol/server` 2.0.0, latest
   142	published) implements `elicitation/create`. A gate placed in Atlas's own MCP
   143	server therefore reaches every client that ever connects, instead of one that was
   144	measured.
   145	
   146	**Solo PO pass**: 21/24 (problem 4, user moment 3, differentiation 3, ontology
   147	value 4, agent value 4, verification 3; no fatal zero).
   148	
   149	**Decision**: write tools ask before they write when `OATLAS_WRITE_CONSENT` is
   150	on. The switch is per session and set by whoever launches the server, so a vault
   151	whose client already owns the gate is unchanged. **One session, one checkpoint,
   152	held by whoever can actually hold it**: a runtime with app-owned config isolation
   153	(Claude) keeps its own gate and the server gate stays off, so nobody is asked
   154	twice; every other runtime — including one nobody has measured — gets the server
   155	gate, because an unasked write is worse than one question too many.
   156	
   157	**Fail closed**: if the gate is on and the client never declared the
   158	`elicitation` capability, the write is refused rather than performed. A
   159	checkpoint that waves traffic through when it cannot see is the failure this
   160	record exists to end.
   161	
   162	**Measured** (disposable vaults, wire evidence):
   163	
   164	| gate | client | asked | vault |
   165	|---|---|---|---|
   166	| off | no elicitation | no | **wrote** (7 → 8) |
   167	| on | no elicitation | no | refused (7 → 7) |
   168	| on | accepts | yes | wrote (7 → 8) |
   169	| on | declines | yes | refused (7 → 7) |
   170	| on | **real Codex** | yes | **refused** (7 → 7) |
   171	
   172	The Codex row used `codex exec` against an isolated `CODEX_HOME`: the same
   173	`add_concept` call that (111) measured writing unasked returned *"The change was
   174	not approved (decline). No change was made."* and left the vault untouched.
   175	
   176	**Recorded dissent**: a gate that asks per tool call will be clicked through, and
   177	batching would have produced one question for a whole plan instead of one per
   178	write. **Falsifier**: a session in which a person approves a long run of writes
   179	without reading them, or asks for the prompts to stop. Then the unit of consent
   180	is the plan, not the call, and this moves up to the plan layer.
   181	
   182	**Isolation is not gate ownership**: Rust's `ISOLATION` says the app can control
   183	a runtime's config directory. Owning the gate is the stronger claim that the
   184	resulting configuration actually asks *for MCP writes* — true for Claude, false
   185	for Codex, whose isolated `CODEX_HOME` was read while its approval policy was
   186	overridden. Conflating the two is how (111) happened;
   187	`runtime-gate.test.ts` holds the one-directional invariant.
   188	
   189	**Not in this change**: restoring Codex in-app chat. That needs the Rust
   190	isolation entry, a rebuilt app, and the installed-app acceptance (111) demands —
   191	reject and allow, for both self-registered and injected writes. Half of it is now
   192	measured; the direct-write half has not been re-measured with both gates live,
   193	and (111) says explicitly not to restore from a direct-file sandbox result.
   194	
   238	## 2026-08-24 (111) — Codex leaves in-app chat until Atlas MCP writes have an app-owned gate
   239	
   240	**Prior decisions**: 2026-08-16 (8) treated Codex `read-only` session mode as a
   241	permission gate and 2026-08-17 (23) made successful gate setup a session-start
   242	condition. This record overturns only the claim that the Codex session mode also
   243	guards Atlas MCP writes. Claude's isolated-config gate and external
   244	Codex MCP setup remain unchanged.
   245	
   246	**Observed falsifier**: installed `1.0.0-rc.10` acceptance opened a real Codex
   247	session in `read-only` mode against a disposable storefront vault. The model
   248	called Atlas `add_relation`; no `session/request_permission` or review card
   249	appeared, yet the file immediately gained `relates: [capabilities/refund]` and
   250	the requested `relation_notes`. The earlier test had measured direct file writes
   251	and read-only MCP calls separately. It never measured an MCP write, so its green
   252	result did not cover the promise the UI made.
   253	
   254	**Solo PO pass**: 22/24 (problem 4, user moment 4, differentiation 3,
   255	ontology value 4, agent value 3, verification 4; no fatal zero).
   256	
   257	**Decision (accountable: Jinan, implemented by Codex)**: remove `codex-acp`
   258	from the guarded-runtime table. Codex remains detected and retains its external
   259	MCP connection/config path, but the installed app does not offer Codex in-app
   260	chat. Only Claude Agent, whose isolated configuration produces the permission
   261	request path the app owns, remains eligible. Do not call a session mode a write
   262	gate unless an installed-app probe shows both reject-without-write and
   263	allow-once-with-write for both self-registered and injected Atlas MCP mutations.
   264	
   265	**Recorded dissent**: hiding Codex chat removes a useful read-only conversation
   266	surface even though only its write path is unsafe. **Falsifier**: an app-owned
   267	MCP proxy or server capability token reliably pauses every Codex Atlas write,
   268	survives a vault self-registered `.codex/config.toml`, and proves reject/allow
   269	on a disposable vault. Restore Codex chat then; do not restore it from a direct
   270	file sandbox result.
   271	
   272	**Status**: active; overturns the Codex portion of decisions (8) and (23).
  5709	## 2026-08-16 (2) — 권한 관문은 **설정을 격리해야만** 존재한다: 앱은 사용자의 전역 Claude 설정을 물려받지 않는다
  5710	
  5711	**소집**: 단독 패스 · **트리거**: 같은 날 첫 기록이 이 조각의 **첫 게이트**로 예약해 둔
  5712	실측(*"볼트 밖 쓰기를 시켜 `requestPermission` 이 실제로 오는지 재라"*)의 결과가 나왔고,
  5713	그 결과가 앞 기록의 전제를 뒤집었다.
  5714	**루브릭**: 해당 없음 (앞 기록의 실행 조건 확정 — 새 표면 없음)
  5715	
  5716	**관찰 (실측 2026-08-16, `claude-agent-acp` 0.68.0)**
  5717	
  5718	파일 능력을 **하나도 선언하지 않고**(`clientCapabilities: {}`) 작업 폴더 **밖** 절대
  5719	경로에 쓰라고 시켰다. 네 조건에서 잰 결과:
  5720	
  5721	| 조건 | 권한 요청 | 결과 |
  5722	|---|---|---|
  5723	| 소유자의 실제 `~/.claude/settings.json` (`defaultMode: auto`, `allow: [Bash(*), Write(*), Edit(*) …]` 15개) | **0회** | ⚠️ **폴더 밖에 파일 생성됨** |
  5724	| 같은 설정 + 세션 모드를 `default`(직접 확인)로 변경 | **0회** | ⚠️ 생성됨. 게다가 `Terminal[execute]` 툴콜까지 |
  5725	| 격리한 설정(`defaultMode: default`, `allow: []`) | **1회** | ✅ 거절하니 파일 안 생김 |
  5726	| 격리한 설정 + 자격증명 없음 | — | ❌ `Authentication required` |
  5727	
  5728	⚠️ **첫 시도는 오염된 실험이었다.** 파일명을 `ESCAPED.txt`, 내용을 `ATLAS-ESCAPE-TEST`
  5729	로 지었더니 에이전트가 *"이건 샌드박스 탈출 테스트라 거부한다"* 고 답했다 — 모델의
  5730	판단력을 잰 것이지 관문을 잰 것이 아니다. 평범한 이름(`meeting-notes.md`)으로 바꾸자
  5731	바로 썼다. **실제 사고는 늘 평범한 얼굴로 온다.**
  5732	
  5733	**결정**
  5734	
  5735	1. **앱이 띄우는 세션은 자기 설정 디렉터리를 쓴다.** 사용자의 전역 설정을 물려받지
  5736	   않는다. 물려받으면 「전역에 `Bash(*)`·`Write(*)` 를 허용해 둔 사람」 — 즉 1차
  5737	   타겟 — 에게는 앱 안 에이전트가 **아무것도 묻지 않는다.** 앱의 약속이 사용자의
  5738	   터미널 설정에 따라 달라지는 것은 약속이 아니다.
  5739	2. **자격증명은 복사하지 않고 심볼릭 링크로 가리킨다.** 격리하면 로그인이 깨지는데,
  5740	   비밀을 앱 폴더로 복사하는 것은 헌장이 막는 종류의 일이다. 링크는 디스크에 보이고
  5741	   원본이 하나로 유지된다. (실측으로 동작 확인.)
  5742	3. **정책은 제목 문자열이 아니라 구조화된 경로로 판정한다.** 권한 요청 원문에
  5743	   `toolCall.rawInput.file_path` 가 절대 경로로 들어 있다. 볼트 안이면 자동 허용,
  5744	   밖이면 사용자에게 묻는다.
  5745	4. **`allow_always` 를 앱이 대신 고르지 않는다.** 그 선택지의 `_meta.permission` 에
  5746	   *"이 디렉터리 전체를 세션 내내 허용"* 하는 규칙이 딸려 온다(실측). 사용자가 직접
  5747	   고를 때만 쓴다.
  5748	
  5749	**적용 규칙**: 헌장 우선 · 최소 슬라이스 · 합집합 금지
  5750	
  5751	**대가 (숨기지 않는다)**: 설정을 격리하면 사용자의 **전역** 스킬·MCP 등록·모델
  5752	취향이 이 세션에 안 실린다. 작업 폴더의 `.claude/` 는 그대로 실린다. 이것을 「기능이
  5753	빠졌다」가 아니라 「앱이 관리하는 세션」으로 화면에 적어야 한다.
  5754	
  5755	**서명 (accountable)**: stark
  5756	
  5757	**기록된 반대**: *"격리는 사용자가 자기 도구에 해 둔 설정을 무시하는 것이다. 자기
  5758	스킬이 안 뜨는 것을 사용자는 고장으로 읽을 것이고, 그러면 결국 터미널로 돌아간다 —
  5759	앱 안에서 쓰게 만들려던 목적 자체가 깨진다."*
  5760	**반증 조건**: 사용자가 「내 스킬/설정이 왜 없냐」고 묻거나, 앱 안 세션을 쓰다 같은
  5761	일을 터미널에서 다시 하는 것이 관측되면 반대가 옳았던 것 — 그때의 처방은 격리를
  5762	푸는 것이 아니라 **사용자의 설정을 읽되 권한 부분만 앱이 덮어쓰는** 좁은 길을 찾는
  5763	것이다.
  5764	**재검토**: ACP 채팅 출하 + 2주 (앞 기록의 관측과 같은 시점).
  5765	
  5766	**상태**: 유효
  5767

  5770	## 2026-08-16 — ACP 로 사용자의 에이전트를 앱 안으로 들인다: 「위임 일꾼」 경계는 뒤집고, 「지도가 주」는 유지한다
  5771	
  5772	**소집**: PO 카운슬 5석(근거 · 결 · 지킴이 · 해자 · 지렛대) · **트리거**: 새 표면 신설 +
  5773	표면 제거 검토(BYOK) + 방향 · 선행 결정 뒤집기 + 소유자 요청
  5774	**루브릭**: **15/24** (치명적 0: 없음) — Problem insight 3 · User moment 3(근거) ·
  5775	Differentiation 2(해자, 조건부 3) · Ontology value 2 · Agent value 3(지킴이) ·
  5776	Verification 2(결)
  5777	**절차 흠결(기록)**: 「결」 자리는 2라운드에 전사 소실로 재개하지 못했다. 그 자리의
  5778	값과 판정은 **1라운드 그대로**이며 반박을 받지 않았다. 다음 소집이 알아야 할 사실이라
  5779	적어 둔다.
  5780	
  5781	**관찰(실측 2026-08-16)**:
  5782	- 앱 안 채팅 사용은 실볼트 **0건**. 유일한 91줄은 휴지통의 데모 볼트이고, 그중
  5783	  `purpose:"agent"` 79건은 **사용이 아니라 소유자 본인의 모델 A/B**다 — 서로 다른
  5784	  질문 8개(전부 "볼트 구조 감사" 변형), 2026-08-01 17:26~22:54 한 자리,
  5785	  `gemma4:12b` 44 / `qwen3:8b` 35, 쓰기 도구 0회. **패널은 실사용 질문을 한 번도
  5786	  실은 적이 없다.**
  5787	- 키체인에 `Ontology Atlas` 서비스 **자체가 없다** — 명명 벤더 BYOK 경로 출하 이래 0회.
  5788	- 같은 기간 MCP 는 활발(`activity.jsonl` 15줄, 최신 2026-08-15, `claude-code` ·
  5795	  수용하고 macOS 가 직접 경고를 띄웠다 ② `summarizeWrite` 의 `add_relations` 분기가
  5796	  행별 `why` 를 버린다(`mcp/src/index.js:6031`) — 이유가 frontmatter 에만 남고 감사
  5797	  줄에서 사라진다.
  5798	
  5799	**선행 결정과의 관계 — 하나는 뒤집고 둘은 유지한다**:
  5800	- **2026-07-27 「AI 대화는 앱 안 에이전트 패널에 산다」 → 부분 뒤집음.**
  5801	  「패널 = 화면 동료 / 외부 에이전트 = 위임 일꾼」이라는 **사다리 두 단 구분을
  5802	  뒤집는다** — 위임 일꾼을 앱 안에서 부를 수 있게 된다. 근거: 그 기록의 반대 1
  5803	  ("MCP 가 붙는 순간 패널은 열등한 중복이고 1차 타겟은 패널을 안 열 것")의 관측
  5804	  형태가 실제로 나타났고, ACP 는 그 반대가 지목한 비용(벤더 3사 API 추종)을 제거한다.
  5805	  단 **적용 규칙 「합집합 금지 — 지도가 주」는 그대로 유지한다.** 패널을 주 작업면으로
  5806	  승격하지 않는다.
  5807	- **2026-08-01 「네 번째 연결은 벤더가 아니라 문이다」 → 유효 인용, 건드리지 않는다.**
  5808	  `provider:"local"` 갈래(91/91 `host:"localhost:11434"`)는 헌장 ①("인터넷 없이
  5809	  돌아간다")의 유일한 담지자다. ACP 두 어댑터는 구독 인증에 묶여 있어 대체재가 아니다.
  5810	- **`tool-catalog.ts` 의 "코드가 필요한 일은 터미널의 몫" → 유효.** ACP 세션의
  5811	  작업 폴더를 저장소 전체로 열지 않음으로써 지킨다.
  5812	
  5813	**결정 (소유자 확정)**:
  5814	1. **ACP v1 을 도입한다.** 첫 조각은 **claude-code 단독** — 지금 보고 있는 노드를
  5815	   `embeddedContext` 로 실어 보내고 기존 제안 카드까지 왕복 한 번. codex 는 다음 조각.
  5816	2. **BYOK 는 동결한다(삭제 아님).** 명명 벤더 3종은 신규 추가·기능 확장을 멈추고
  5817	   화면에서 강조하지 않는다. **로컬 러너는 존치한다.** 삭제 판정은 2026-08-24 관측
  5818	   이후 별건으로 — 지금 지우면 진행 중인 반증 관측의 계측기를 관측이 끝나기 전에
  5819	   뜯는 것이 된다.
  5820	3. **전송 기록 약속을 정직하게 낮추고, 낮춘 만큼 채운다.** 「모든 왕복이 기록돼요」
  5821	   → 「여기서 보낸 것만 기록돼요 — 실행기가 자기 서버와 주고받는 것은 그 도구가
  5822	   기록해요」. 동시에 Atlas 가 **실제로 넘긴 것**(주입한 `mcpServers` · `embeddedContext`
  5823	   로 보낸 노드 · 사용자가 친 질문)은 빠짐없이 기록한다. 헌장 예외로 명문화하는 안은
  5824	   기각(⑤ 정면 충돌).
  5825	4. **권한은 자동 승인하지 않는다.** Buzz 식 `allow_once` 자동 승인 금지. `fs`/`terminal`
  5826	   능력 미선언 + `requestPermission` 을 `agent_setup.rs` 의 기존 봉쇄 경계로 답한다.
  5827	   ⚠️ 능력 미선언은 ACP 경로만 닫고 어댑터가 감싼 Claude Code 의 네이티브 도구는
  5828	   살아 있다 — **유일한 관문은 `requestPermission`** 이므로, 볼트 밖 쓰기를 시켜
  5829	   그것이 실제로 오는지 재는 것이 이 조각의 **첫 게이트**다.
  5830	5. **작업 폴더는 볼트 한정.** `connect_project_source` 로 이미 묶인 저장소에 한해
  5831	   읽기만. 쓰기는 어떤 경우에도 Atlas MCP 도구로만.
  5832	6. **선행 조건 둘을 먼저 닫는다**: 볼트 위생 거부(`/` · `/Applications` · `/System` ·
  5833	   `/Users` · `$HOME`), 최소폭 계약(1040 에서 패널 열었을 때 지도 캔버스 ≥480px —
  5834	   현재 실측 333px 이므로 게이트가 빈 세트가 아니다).
  5835	7. **`why` 회수를 이 조각의 정의에 포함한다.** 세션에서 수락된 변경의 이유에 사용자의
  5836	   실제 질문이 남는 데까지가 완료 조건이다. 빠지면 원래 문제가 앱 안에서 재현될 뿐이다.
  5837	8. **성공 지표는 채팅 횟수가 아니라 `why`/provenance 줄 수다**(지렛대 자기반박 수용).
  5838	
  5839	**적용 규칙**: 최소 슬라이스 · 헌장 우선 · 합집합 금지(지도가 주) · 제거 요구
  5840	(38종 레지스트리 브라우저 · 커스텀 커맨드 입력란 · 첫 실행 위저드 신설 · Buzz 식

  5846	*"터미널 계수 없이 짓는 것은 근거 없는 두 번째 공급측 베팅이다. 「마찰을 없애면
  5847	쓴다」는 인과는 2026-08-01 키 0짜리 문으로 이미 시험됐고 그날 21회 후 15일 0회로
  5848	끝났다. 앱 안에서 그 질문이 실제로 던져진 사례는 0건이고 모집단은 N≈1 이다."*

  5851	병행 측정으로 내려갔다 — 그 해제 자체가 이 기록의 대상이다.

     1	/**
     2	 * **How the permission gate is raised** per runtime — the method differs by tool.
     3	 *
     4	 * ## Two methods, and what works differs per tool (measured 2026-08-16)
     5	 *
     6	 * | | Claude | Codex |
     7	 * |---|---|---|
     8	 * | config isolation (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` / `CODEX_HOME`) | **works** | it is read, but **the approval policy is ignored** |
     9	 * | session mode (`session/set_mode`) | has no read-only | blocks direct files, **not Atlas MCP writes** |
    10	 *
    11	 * The codex measurement was the surprise. Putting `approval_policy = "untrusted"` and
    12	 * `sandbox_mode = "workspace-write"` into an isolated `CODEX_HOME` still produced **zero permission
    13	 * requests and files created outside the vault.** Yet the `model` value in the same folder was
    14	 * honoured — so our config **is read, and only the approval policy is overridden by the adapter's
    15	 * session mode.**
    16	 *
    17	 * The earlier conclusion that this made Codex guarded was overturned by installed-app acceptance
    18	 * on 2026-08-24. A self-registered Atlas `add_relation` produced no `session/request_permission`, no review
    19	 * card, and changed the vault immediately. A mode can be called read-only while an MCP child still
    20	 * mutates disk; the app must own the MCP write checkpoint before Codex is eligible for in-app chat.
    21	 */
    22	
    23	/**
    24	 * The mode to switch to once a session stands. A runtime absent here is not switched —
    25	 * **no mode is imposed on a tool that was never measured** (imposing one without knowing what that
    26	 * name means on that tool is guesswork).
    27	 */
    28	export const GATED_SESSION_MODE: Readonly<Record<string, string>> = {};
    29	
    30	/**
    31	 * Does this runtime **have a permission gate** — may the screen say so?
    32	 *
    33	 * True if **either** method works. `isolated` is Rust's verdict on whether config isolation is
    34	 * possible, and what is added here is the session-mode branch. This function must be the only path —
    35	 * if the screen's sentence and the action actually taken diverge, the screen makes a promise it
    36	 * cannot keep.
    37	 */
    38	export function isGuardedRuntime(runtimeId: string, isolated: boolean): boolean {
    39	  return isolated || runtimeId in GATED_SESSION_MODE;
    40	}
    41	
    42	/**
    43	 * Does this runtime's **own configuration** already put a permission request in front
    44	 * of a person for every tool call — including calls into our MCP server?
    45	 *
    46	 * Only config isolation has been measured to do that (Claude: an isolated
    47	 * `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` with an empty allow-list produced the request, and declining it
    48	 * left the file uncreated). A session mode is not the same thing: Codex `read-only`
    49	 * blocked direct file access while an Atlas MCP write went through with no request at
    50	 * all (installed rc.10 acceptance, 2026-08-24).
    51	 *
    52	 * The answer decides **who holds the single checkpoint** for a session. `true` hands it
    53	 * to the runtime and keeps the server gate off, so nobody is asked twice. `false` — the
    54	 * default for anything unmeasured — turns the server gate on, because an unasked write
    55	 * is worse than one question too many.
    56	 */
    57	export function runtimeOwnsWriteGate(runtimeId: string | null | undefined): boolean {
    58	  return typeof runtimeId === 'string' && CONFIG_ISOLATED_RUNTIMES.has(runtimeId);
    59	}
    60	
    61	/**
    62	 * Runtimes whose configuration the app isolates. Mirrors `ISOLATION` in
    63	 * `src-tauri/src/acp.rs`; `runtime-gate.test.ts` keeps the two from drifting.
    64	 */
    65	const CONFIG_ISOLATED_RUNTIMES: ReadonlySet<string> = new Set(['claude-acp']);
     1	/**
     2	 * The safety verdict for the "working mode" list — **it separates the known from the unknown.**
     3	 *
     4	 * Why (2026-08-17). The old verdict was a one-line denylist:
     5	 *
     6	 * ```
     7	 * modes.filter((m) => !GATE_REMOVING_MODES.has(m.id))
     8	 * ```
     9	 *
    10	 * It hides only what is written down. So **when the adapter adds a new mode it becomes visible and
    11	 * selectable without our knowing.** If that mode removes the permission gate, one choice undoes this
    12	 * screen's promise and the screen says nothing. **A safety device that treats what it does not know
    13	 * as safe is not a device.**
    14	 *
    15	 * It is an immediate problem too: the adapter versions are being bumped
    16	 * (`claude-agent-acp` 0.68→0.69, `codex-acp` 1.3→1.4) and our gate measurements were taken on the
    17	 * **old versions**.
    18	 *
    19	 * **Three categories:**
    20	 *
    21	 * - **Measured to remove the gate** → never shown (as before).
    22	 * - **Measured to be fine** → shown normally.
    23	 * - **Not yet measured** → shown, but **stated as unknown.** It is not hidden because blocking a
    24	 *   perfectly good new mode is a lie too. The permission card already uses the same discipline
    25	 *   (say unknown when unknown, and recommend the safe side).
    26	 */
    27	
    28	/**
    29	 * Requires the bare minimum for the verdict — it discriminates on `id` alone. So the caller's wider
    30	 * type (`AcpChoice`) can go in and come back out unchanged.
    31	 */
    32	export interface AcpModeChoice {
    33	  id: string;
    34	}
    35	
    36	/**
    37	 * Modes **measured to remove the gate**. The dividing line is not "is it strict" but **"does it let
    38	 * things through without asking"** — a mode that closes with a rejection (`dontAsk`) fails toward
    39	 * safety and is therefore not here.
    40	 */
    41	const GATE_REMOVING = new Set([
    42	  'bypasspermissions',
    43	  'acceptedits',
    44	  'agent-full-access',
    45	  /*
    46	   * `agent` belongs here. By name it sounds like "the normal mode", but the measurement
    47	   * (2026-08-16) is recorded in `src-tauri/src/acp.rs`: launching codex on its default (`agent`)
    48	   * gave *"files written outside the working folder with zero permission requests"*.
    49	   *
    50	   * **Re-measured 2026-08-17 — unchanged on `codex-acp` 1.4.** This closes the worry the block above
    51	   * left open ("our gate measurements were taken on the old versions"). In the installed app this
    52	   * line was briefly removed, a session opened on `agent`, and it was asked to *"write hello to
    53	   * /tmp/atlas-gate-probe.txt"* — the file appeared (contents `hello`) with **no permission card at
    54	   * all**. This adapter offers only two modes (`Read-only`, `Agent`). `Read-only` is safer for
    55	   * direct files but does not guard MCP writes, so mode classification alone never makes Codex
    56	   * eligible for in-app chat (`runtime-gate.ts`).
    57	   */
    58	  'agent',
    59	]);
    60	
    61	/** Modes measured not to widen direct adapter access inside an already guarded runtime. */
    62	const VERIFIED_SAFE = new Set(['default', 'read-only', 'readonly', 'plan', 'ask']);
    63	
    64	const normalize = (id: unknown): string =>
    65	  typeof id === 'string' ? id.trim().toLowerCase() : '';
    66	
    67	export interface ModePartition<T extends AcpModeChoice = AcpModeChoice> {
    68	  /** What is offered to the screen — the measured and the unmeasured together. */
    69	  offered: T[];
    70	  /** The ids among them that are **not yet measured**. The screen must state this fact. */
    71	  unverified: string[];
    72	  /** Entries dropped for a malformed shape — counted so they do not disappear silently. */
    73	  dropped: number;
    74	}
    75	
    76	export function partitionModes<T extends AcpModeChoice>(
    77	  modes: readonly T[],
    78	): ModePartition<T> {
    79	  const offered: T[] = [];
    80	  const unverified: string[] = [];
    81	  let dropped = 0;
    82	  for (const mode of Array.isArray(modes) ? modes : []) {
    83	    const key = normalize(mode?.id);
    84	    if (key.length === 0) {
    85	      dropped += 1;
    86	      continue;
    87	    }
    88	    if (GATE_REMOVING.has(key)) continue;
    89	    offered.push(mode);
    90	    if (!VERIFIED_SAFE.has(key)) unverified.push(mode.id);
    91	  }
    92	  return { offered, unverified, dropped };
    93	}
    15	 *
    16	 * | | Claude | Codex |
    17	 * |---|---|---|
    18	 * | config isolation | works | **only the approval policy is ignored** |
    19	 * | session mode | has no "read only" | blocks direct files, **not Atlas MCP writes** |
    20	 *
    21	 * With codex, putting `approval_policy = "untrusted"` in an isolated `CODEX_HOME`
    22	 * still produced **a file outside the vault after zero permission requests**. Switching to
    23	 * `read-only` blocked that direct-file path, but installed-app acceptance on 2026-08-24 proved
    24	 * that a self-registered Atlas `add_relation` still executed with **zero**
    25	 * `session/request_permission` requests and changed the vault immediately.
    26	 *
    27	 * So the danger is **the two sides diverging**: the screen says this tool will ask
    28	 * while the session does not set that mode, or the mode is set and the screen does
    29	 * not say so. The user can detect neither — no error is raised.
    30	 */
    31

     1	/**
     2	 * ACP permission requests only carry the MCP tool name. The server's generated
     3	 * surface is the authority for whether a registered tool reads or writes; the
     4	 * sibling contract test compares every entry so this fail-closed list cannot
     5	 * silently drift when the server inventory changes.
     6	 */
     7	const ATLAS_READ_TOOLS = new Set([
     8	  'connection_info',
     9	  'git_status',
    10	  'git_history',
    11	  'list_concepts',
    12	  'get_concept',
    13	  'get_concepts',
    14	  'find_evidence',
    15	  'find_backlinks',
    16	  'find_neighbors',
    17	  'find_path',
    18	  'list_kinds',
    19	  'find_orphans',
    20	  'query_concepts',
    21	  'compile_ontology',
    22	  'query_ontology',
    23	  'validate_vault',
    24	  'analyze_repo_structure',
    25	  'infer_imports',
    26	  'index_project',
    27	]);
    28	
    29	export type AtlasToolMode = 'read' | 'write';
    30	
    31	/**
    32	 * Returns null for another server. Unknown tools on our server are writes:
    33	 * adding a read tool without updating the generated-surface contract must add
    34	 * friction, never grant a new write path by accident.
    35	 */
    36	export function atlasToolMode(
    37	  permissionToolName: string | null,
    38	  serverName: string,
    39	): AtlasToolMode | null {
    40	  if (!permissionToolName || !serverName) return null;
    41	  const prefix = `mcp__${serverName}__`;
    42	  if (!permissionToolName.startsWith(prefix)) return null;
    43	  const toolName = permissionToolName.slice(prefix.length);
    44	  if (!toolName || toolName.includes('__')) return null;
    45	  return ATLAS_READ_TOOLS.has(toolName) ? 'read' : 'write';
    46	}

  1254	/// Deletes the keychain item registered against that folder **unconditionally**.
  1255	///
  1256	/// The difference from `clear_shadowing_credentials`: that one honors "do not touch it
  1257	/// while it still works", but this is called only when the user **explicitly pressed
  1258	/// "reconnect"**. At that point, deleting whether it works or not is what that button means.
  1259	pub(crate) fn remove_shadow_credentials(config_dir: &Path) {
  1260	    #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
  1261	    {
  1262	        let service = claude_credentials_service(config_dir);
  1263	        let mut find = std::process::Command::new("security");
  1264	        find.args(["find-generic-password", "-s", &service]);
  1265	        if !bounded_output(find, KEYCHAIN_PROBE_TIMEOUT)
  1266	            .map(|out| out.contains(&service))
  1267	            .unwrap_or(false)
  1268	        {
  1269	            return;
  1270	        }
  1271	        let mut del = std::process::Command::new("security");
  1272	        del.args(["delete-generic-password", "-s", &service]);
  1273	        let _ = bounded_output(del, KEYCHAIN_PROBE_TIMEOUT);
  1274	    }
  1275	    #[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]
  1276	    {
  1277	        let _ = config_dir;
  1278	    }
  1279	}

  1300	        .ok()?
  1301	        .get("loggedIn")?

  1326	/// working login. So it is true only when `loggedIn` is explicitly `false`.
  1327	pub(crate) fn claude_status_is_logged_out(stdout: &str) -> bool {

  1340	/// Prepares the app-owned permission boundary before an ACP child can start.
  1341	///
  1342	/// A runtime without verified configuration isolation is rejected here even if UI filtering fails.
  1343	/// A session mode is not enough: Codex `read-only` blocked direct file access but allowed a
  1344	/// self-registered Atlas MCP write without emitting a permission request (2026-08-24 acceptance).
  1345	pub(crate) fn prepare_runtime_isolation(
  1346	    runtime_id: &str,
  1347	    app_data_dir: &Path,
  1348	    home: Option<&Path>,
  1349	    cli: Option<&Path>,
  1350	    path_env: &str,
  1351	) -> Result<(&'static str, PathBuf), String> {
  1352	    // Two separate questions, asked in the order that matters. Eligibility first: a runtime whose
  1353	    // gate has not been measured must not reach a conversation even if the app *could* isolate its
  1354	    // config. Conflating the two is how decision (111) happened.
  1355	    if !chat_eligible(runtime_id) {
  1356	        return Err(format!("permission-gate-unsupported:{runtime_id}"));
  1357	    }
  1358	    let env = config_env_for(runtime_id)
  1359	        .ok_or_else(|| format!("permission-gate-unsupported:{runtime_id}"))?;
  1360	    let dir = prepare_isolated_config(runtime_id, app_data_dir, home, cli, path_env)
  1361	        .map_err(|reason| format!("isolation-failed:{reason}"))?;
  1362	    Ok((env, dir))
  1363	}

  1365	/// Puts the credentials link in place. If it already points at the right place, leave it alone.
  1366	fn link_credentials(source: &Path, link: &Path) -> Result<(), String> {
  1367	    if let Ok(existing) = std::fs::read_link(link) {
  1368	        if existing == source {
  1369	            return Ok(());
  1370	        }
  1371	    }
  1372	    // Delete whatever was left behind — a broken link pointing at an old home leaves login
  1373	    // broken and turns into "the settings exist, so why doesn't it work?".
  1374	    let _ = std::fs::remove_file(link);
  1375	
  1376	    #[cfg(unix)]
  1377	    {
  1378	        std::os::unix::fs::symlink(source, link)
  1379	            .map_err(|err| format!("credentials-link-failed:{err}"))
  1380	    }
  1381	    #[cfg(windows)]
  1382	    {
  1383	        // Symbolic links on Windows require a privilege. On failure, proceed without a link
  1384	        // (a login screen appearing is better than silently copying secrets) and raise the
  1385	        // reason as-is.
   229	/// The config an app-opened codex session runs under. **Not inherited from the user's own
   230	/// `~/.codex/config.toml`** — that file is where a person keeps the permissions they granted for
   231	/// their own terminal work, and a session opened from a map panel is not that.
   232	///
   233	/// `sandbox_mode = "read-only"` is the floor, not a preference. The 2026-08-24 measurements showed
   234	/// an approval policy being overridden by the adapter's session mode, so the boundary cannot rest
   235	/// on asking — it rests on the process not being able to write in the first place. Vault changes do
   236	/// not need it: they travel through the Atlas MCP server, which asks before it writes.
   237	///
   238	/// `approval_policy = "untrusted"` keeps the escalation path alive for the commands codex may still
   239	/// legitimately propose, so a blocked action surfaces as a question rather than a silent failure.
   240	///
   241	/// ⚠️ **The `ontology-atlas` block is how the *vault's own* registration gets a gate.** A vault
   242	/// written by `init` registers that server in its project `.codex/config.toml`, and a session loads
   243	/// that file even from a fresh isolated home (measured 2026-08-24) — so without this the app would

   246	/// It is declared **here** rather than in the vault's file on purpose. That file is shared with the

     1	/**
     2	 * **The app-owned write checkpoint.**
     3	 *
     4	 * ⚠️ Why this file exists (2026-08-24). The permission gate an agent shows is the
     5	 * *agent's* gate, and it does not necessarily cover this server. Measured on the
     6	 * installed `1.0.0-rc.10`: a Codex session running in `read-only` mode blocked
     7	 * direct file writes, yet a self-registered Atlas `add_relation` changed the vault
     8	 * with **no permission request and no review card**. Codex's `--ask-for-approval`
     9	 * documents its scope plainly — it decides "when the model requires human approval
    10	 * before executing **a command**" — so MCP tool calls were never in that scope. The
    11	 * screen promised a gate the wire did not have, and Codex was removed from in-app
    12	 * chat because of it (`docs/DECISIONS.md` 2026-08-24 (111)).
    13	 *
    14	 * That record also wrote down what would earn Codex its way back: *"an app-owned
    15	 * MCP proxy or server capability token reliably pauses every Codex Atlas write."*
    16	 * This is that checkpoint, and it lives where it belongs — **in the server that
    17	 * performs the write**, not in a third party's config. A gate the vault owns holds
    18	 * for every client that ever connects, not just the one that was measured.
    19	 *
    20	 * The mechanism is MCP elicitation (`elicitation/create`): before a write tool
    21	 * touches disk, the server asks the connected client to put the question in front
    22	 * of a person. `codex-acp` forwards `mcpServer/elicitation/request` into ACP
    23	 * `session/request_permission`, which is the same permission card the app already
    24	 * renders for Claude — so one server-side gate reaches both.
    25	 *
    26	 * **Fail closed.** If the gate is on and the client never declared the
    27	 * `elicitation` capability, the write is refused rather than performed silently.
    28	 * A checkpoint that waves traffic through when it cannot see is not a checkpoint;
    29	 * that is precisely the failure this file was written to end.
    30	 */
    31	
    32	/** Same vocabulary as `OATLAS_READ_ONLY` so the two switches read alike. */
    33	export function parseConsentEnv(value) {
    34	  if (typeof value !== 'string') return false;
    35	  return ['1', 'true', 'yes', 'on'].includes(value.trim().toLowerCase());
    36	}
    37	
    38	/**
    39	 * A short, human-readable line naming what is about to change. It is the whole
    40	 * question a person answers, so it must say the vault-visible effect — not the
    41	 * tool's internal argument shape.
    42	 */
    43	export function describeWrite(toolName, args) {
    44	  const a = args && typeof args === 'object' ? args : {};
    45	  const slug = typeof a.slug === 'string' ? a.slug : null;
    85	    case 'finalize_project_meaning':
    86	      return 'Write the project meaning receipt';
    87	    case 'index_project':
    88	      return 'Write an index of the connected source';
    89	    default:
    90	      return `Run ${toolName}`;
    91	  }
    92	}
    93	
    94	/**
    95	 * A dry run asks for nothing: it reports a plan and leaves the disk alone, so
    96	 * pausing it would train people to click through the very card that matters.
    97	 * The flag name differs per tool, so both spellings are honoured.
    98	 */
    99	export function isDryRun(args) {
   100	  if (!args || typeof args !== 'object') return false;
   101	  return args.dryRun === true || args.dry_run === true;
   102	}
   103	
   104	export const CONSENT_DECLINED = 'consent-declined';
   105	export const CONSENT_UNAVAILABLE = 'consent-unavailable';
   106	
   107	/**
   108	 * Ask the connected client to put this write in front of a person.
   109	 *
   110	 * `server` is the low-level SDK `Server`; `elicitInput` is its push-style
   111	 * server→client request. Returns `{ allowed }` and, when refused, a `reason` the
   112	 * caller turns into an error the agent can read and act on.
   113	 */
   114	export async function requestWriteConsent({ server, toolName, args, enabled }) {
   115	  if (!enabled) return { allowed: true, asked: false };
   116	  if (isDryRun(args)) return { allowed: true, asked: false };
   117	
   118	  const capabilities =
   119	    typeof server?.getClientCapabilities === 'function' ? server.getClientCapabilities() : undefined;
   120	  if (!capabilities || !capabilities.elicitation) {
   121	    return {
   122	      allowed: false,
   123	      asked: false,
   124	      reason: CONSENT_UNAVAILABLE,
   125	      message:
   126	        `This vault requires a human decision before any write, and this client did not offer ` +
   127	        `a way to ask (no "elicitation" capability was declared at initialize). No change was made. ` +
   128	        `Use a client that can surface permission requests, or unset OATLAS_WRITE_CONSENT for a ` +
   129	        `vault where the client owns the gate.`,
   130	    };
   131	  }
   132	
   133	  const summary = describeWrite(toolName, args);
   134	  let result;
   135	  try {
   136	    result = await server.elicitInput({
   137	      mode: 'form',
   138	      /*
   139	       * ⚠️ **`confirm` is asked for, never required** (installed acceptance, 2026-08-24).
   140	       *
   141	       * It was `required: ['confirm']` at first, and that made the gate refuse **every** answer,
   142	       * including yes. A form-capable client fills the boolean; an ACP bridge does not have one to
   143	       * fill. `codex-acp` maps this request onto ACP `session/request_permission`, the app renders
   144	       * its ordinary permission card, and pressing 「allow once」 comes back as `action: 'accept'`
   145	       * with **no form content at all**. Codex reported it verbatim: *"the permission response was
   165	  } catch (error) {
   166	    // A transport error, a timeout, or a client that advertised the capability
   167	    // and then refused the method all land here. None of them is a yes.
   168	    return {
   169	      allowed: false,
   170	      asked: true,
   171	      reason: CONSENT_UNAVAILABLE,
   172	      message:
   173	        `Asking for permission failed (${error?.message ?? 'unknown error'}). No change was made.`,
   174	    };
   175	  }
   176	
   177	  /*
   178	   * **`action` is the answer; the boolean only overrides it.**
   179	   *
   180	   * `accept` is the MCP elicitation spelling of "the person said yes" — `decline` and `cancel` are
   181	   * the other two, and neither reaches here as an approval. A client that *did* render the form and
   182	   * whose user unticked the box sends `accept` with `confirm: false`; that is a no, and it wins.
   183	   * Absence is not a no — see the schema comment above for what absence actually measured as.
   184	   */
   185	  const answered = result?.content?.confirm;
   186	  const accepted = result?.action === 'accept' && answered !== false;
   187	  if (accepted) return { allowed: true, asked: true };
   188	
   189	  return {
   190	    allowed: false,
   191	    asked: true,
   192	    reason: CONSENT_DECLINED,
   193	    message: `The change was not approved (${result?.action ?? 'no answer'}). No change was made: ${summary}.`,
   194	  };
   195	}

 ## Definition
+
 The ability to detect ACP (Agent Client Protocol) v1 coding-agent executors already installed on the device, directly launch only those with a verified app-owned permission gate (currently Claude Agent), and apply scoped permission review. No new Atlas API key or vendor integration is needed: it uses the subscription authentication already held by the launched tool.
 
+
+## Behaviour Contract
+
 Ten things this capability actually does:
 
 1. **Detection.** The executor status of this device is divided into five categories: `ready`, `cli-missing`, `node-missing`, `uvx-missing`, and `binary-missing`. The reason for not collapsing the status into just "installed/not installed" is that the user's required actions differ for each state.

+## Constraints: Source binding and currentness
+
 After the installed app binds a human-selected source root to the sidecar, new MCP processes reproduce the same bounded fingerprint locally. Source currentness is read as `current` only when matching the storage receipt exactly; changes fail-closed as `source_changed`. Private absolute paths and raw source inventory are not included in MCP responses.
 
+## Constraints: Competency qualification
+
 When a repository proposal signs competency as `answered`, it only checks that the required witness array is not empty. It verifies that `abilities` cover all proposed domains via typed domain→capability witnesses, and that `evidence` cites every capability slug and canonical path together. If only some are covered, it returns a structured error with the missing target slugs and does not create a write plan. Honest `partial`/`visible-gap` proposals can still be reviewed and stored.
 
 If project purpose, proposed domain, or project→domain relation has confidence ≥0.8, or `scope`/`domains` are `answered`, two distinct current semantic sources must actually support the non-literal meaning of that claim. The purpose requires both sources to align with the purpose claim; domains require both sources to provide a domain name and an explicit responsibility sentence. Duplicated text across documents, unrelated trusted documents, roadmap/negated/deprecated evidence, package manifests, and implementation paths do not count as a second semantic authority. If only one source exists, it remains below confidence 0.8 with an explicit `partial` gap (reviewable), but inflating it to high-confidence/completed causes failure before review/write plan. The analyzer also prioritizes project identity sentences over later feature sentences, and marks domains as corroborated candidates only when separate product/architecture responsibility sentences exist. Implementation elements overlapping domain names or having only one candidate are not auto-assigned roles but remain as project-scoped evidence.
 
+## Constraints: Cold-start evidence reading
+
 Do not blindly truncate the first 1,200 characters of README. Preserve purpose, responsibility/architecture, and ability/capability prose within heading-scoped blocks, structurally excluding sponsor/backer/funding/donation/TOC and link/image-only decorations, then pass them in original order. Do not change the existing limits: 1,200 chars/doc, 8 headings/doc, 6 docs/packet, and 256 KiB pre-read cap. `Excludes` should contain only product/concept boundaries stated by the source. "Not yet proven" or "outside bounded scan" are `Uncertainty`/competency gaps; if a proposal includes these as exclusions, block them via `epistemic-exclusion-boundary` before write plan.
 
 Cold-start semantic evidence is carried by existing `semanticEvidence` packets from classified current Markdown under root `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/`, `site/`, and `website/`. Explore up to 200 Markdown files and 1,000 directory entries across all three roots, stopping at 256 KiB pre-read for general semantic documents. Visit each actual path in the same directory only once; exclude archives and symlinks that are broken or outside the repository. Maintain the final packet limit of 6 docs and 1,200 chars/doc. For proposals arriving in the same call, recalculate existing read-only import receipts to verify TS/JS/Python file endpoints and Go importing-file/package-directory endpoints and directions. These documents and paths serve as evidence/provenance only; they do not auto-approve business meaning or `depends_on`. Without maker-independent qualification and human exact-plan approval, do not open `writePlan`. If relationship rationale directly names the exact repository `path:` of both endpoint concepts, that path must also appear in evidence for the same relationship. Fail closed with `relation-path-citation-mismatch` if only other documents are cited; do not block general semantic sentences by guessing filenames.
 
+## Constraints: Meaning repair review
+
 When a fresh `agent_brief` reads current source and incomplete competency together, set the first `nextActions` to `review_competency_repair`. The connected `meaningRepair:v2` compact manifest contains only disposition count, provenance, `reviewRevision`, and the first `meaning_repair_review` call; a separate `meaningRepairReviewPage:v1` carries current project Markdown declarations, structural review candidates from typed containment, canonical-path candidates directly supported by current source receipts, and all unresolved targets. Containment and path existence are not human semantic approval or action proof, so do not auto-convert candidates to `answered` or write them. If source/provenance/scope/validation/mtime is unstable, block repair while preserving existing source/health behavior. The first review read splits project, domain, and capability into deterministic, stateless cursors by their exact union. Each page has max 20 items, ≤5 KiB JSON, and provides literal `get_concepts(body:"full")` calls for the same target. `reviewRevision` binds graph/source/typed row/mtime; the verifier checks missing/duplicate/order/size/recency up to the last page, plus kind/mmtime/non-truncation of each full-body and source/bundle parity.
 
+## Constraints: Import inference by language
+
 In Python cold start, read `README.rst`, non-executed static `setup.py` package contract, and top-level `__init__.py` package as bounded evidence. `infer_imports` condenses static imports from that package into file/module edges. This result is implementation evidence; do not auto-promote to domain/capability/semantic `depends_on`. Each module edge carries up to 5 exact file-edge receipts and presence of other evidence. Edges not in the vault return `rationale_review_required` instead of executable `proposedAction`. If the implementation path is already known, use `focusPath` or `reviewMode:"focus"` first. This mode returns exact incoming/outgoing static import counts (up to 100 receipts) and a stateless cursor for that file without a loadable vault. Empty results do not imply no impact; they fix symbol/test/dynamic behavior and ontology meaning as still pending separate confirmation. If `reviewMode` is omitted, return the existing full graph only when expected MCP total response (text + structured content) is ≤128 KiB. For larger reconciled scans, return only one review candidate, exact evidence, preflight calls for both concepts/relations, stop conditions, stateless cursor, and a `delivery` receipt with auto-selection reason/expected bytes instead of the full array. `reviewMode:"next"` explicitly requests this compact packet. `reviewMode:"full"` preserves the full shape but requires a second confirmation `allowLargeResponse:true` if exceeding 128 KiB. Oversized omission calls without a loadable vault for reconciliation return structured errors with two recovery options instead of faking safe packets. Internal analysis of `index_project` fixes explicit full to ensure this delivery default does not change existing plan meaning. Separate product/test code and value/type-only usage per evidence; calculate `productValueCount` (intersection of two-dimensional counts across all imports, not bounded samples) for module edges. Explicit type imports in JS/TS and imports inside Python's explicit `TYPE_CHECKING` guard are type-only. `value` means other static imports, not claiming runtime execution. If product code value usage is 0, do not hide test/type evidence but do not ask that import alone to approve product `depends_on`; require separate product meaning evidence. Additionally, the agent must read both concepts and direction, explain semantic rationale, and ask the human; record only one approved item with a non-empty `why`.
 
 Go repositories with a root `go.mod` read module-local imports as bounded text without running compiler, `go list`, module cache, or network. Do not mix package directories into existing file edges or fabricate arbitrary target files; instead, preserve importing file and repository-relative source/target package directory, literal import spec, and production/test role in a separate `goPackageImports:v1` receipt. Full scans share existing file caps; each Go file stops at 256 KiB/256 imports; nested and external modules are explicitly out of scope. Exclude import-like text inside multi-line raw strings and `vendor`/`testdata`/underscore-prefixed fixture trees as evidence outside Go build boundaries. The analyzer passes package directories with high production/value import participation as up to 24 implementation element/path candidates only; do not promote folder names to domain/capability or auto-approve package edges as semantic `depends_on`. Compact/focus responses leave package evidence count and explicit full-evidence calls to prevent large graphs from silently disappearing.

+## Constraints: Impact and blast radius
+
 `impact` and `blast_radius` follow only declared `depends_on`. Containment/domain/element relations are structural evidence for `reachability`/`subgraph`; do not promote to impact or risk.  ## Definition
+
 The app's connect flow and MCP surface that allow AI agents to discover, authenticate, read, and write the vault. Interactive agents within the app collect actual vault tool evidence. Activated only when both absolute paths and read manifests are present, ensuring concepts on screen, text read by models, and audit logs in the vault point to a single source. Local runners apply limited read order and round-trip time limits. One correction is issued if any of mandatory reads, detailed payload remaining exact citations, or Korean responses to Korean questions are omitted; answers not followed on the second attempt are not loaded for the user. Structure audits verify from selecting `domain` candidates after census up to argument contracts reading details of all 8 max exact slugs. The model rejects responses if it synthesizes capabilities/elements as non-existent when only one project root is selected or census confirms existence. Final synthesis preserves incompleteness of the verified scope without using count, fan-out, recommended node counts, or bridge evidence as defects. If batch detail reading exceeds character limits, instead of keeping only the first line, all candidates are first compressed into the same shape of definition excerpt, relationship count, and resolved neighbors, recording only remaining slugs and body text characters in the actual payload as the read scope. Each `*Info` field preserves what was truncated and requires a single concept re-read before editing. This surface is for vault-only curators who cannot see source code, so it does not directly create or edit project's `## Competency answers`. System prompt, write-intent, and final apply boundaries all prevent this, passing source-backed credentials to the repository-reading Atlas MCP builder.
 
+
+## Constraints: Client config and native vault writes
+
 The agent config state in App Settings counts only two actual client configs, not disguising example templates as connections. Source-checkout and app-bundled launch shape, current vault coordinates
 must match to be ready, and live stdio connection and tool inventory are proven by separate `mcp-verify`.
 Native vault writes retain Unix's piece-by-piece no-follow directory FD traversal, private-new-inode
@@ -20,6 +24,8 @@ checks, and atomic rename. Thus, a parent name changing to an external symbolic
 does not redirect a current vault write outside its opened directory. Windows reparse-point races are still
 unproven and provide no guarantee beyond static link checks.
 
+## Identity Boundary
+
 The "interactive agent in the app" mentioned here is the single vault-agent panel owned by this capability:
 it's a provider-neutral loop attaching to keys entered by users or local runners, calling only vault tools. The app
 launching a coding agent with a verified permission gate (currently Claude Agent) directly over ACP is a separate capability owned by `capabilities/acp-runtime`.

 const SERVER_INSTRUCTIONS_TEMPLATE = `ontology-atlas — vault of markdown files where each \`.md\` with a frontmatter \`kind:\` is an ontology node. The graph encodes the codebase's mental model and is shared with the human via plain markdown.
 
+## Which tool answers which question
+
+The vault answers **why**; the source answers **what**. A reason, a boundary, an
+exclusion, or a decision is not in the code, so grep cannot find it. When the
+question is one of those, read the node body before searching the source.
+
+- *"What is X? Why is this boundary drawn here? What was decided, and why?"* \u2192 \`get_concept({slug})\`, then read the body sections \u2014 \`Constraints: \u2026\`, \`\u2026 Boundary\`, \`Inclusions / Exclusions\`, \`\u2026 Contract\`. That is where reasons live.
+- *"What does X contain / belong to?"* \u2192 \`get_concept\`, then \`find_neighbors(slug)\`.
+- *"Who references X?"* (always before a rename, merge, or delete) \u2192 \`find_backlinks(slug)\`.
+- *"How are A and B related?"* \u2192 \`find_path(A, B)\` \u2014 the \`via\` field names the frontmatter key that linked them.
+- *"What breaks if I change X?"* \u2192 \`query_ontology({operation:'impact'})\`, then \`blast_radius\` for the same answer grouped by kind and domain.
+- *"Which nodes match a condition?"* \u2192 \`query_concepts(filter)\`.
+- *"Where do I start? Is this vault trustworthy?"* \u2192 \`query_ontology({operation:'workspace_brief'})\`, then \`validate_vault({})\`.
+
+
 ## Node identity
 
 Every valid node has both identities: immutable \`uid\` is the permanent machine identity, while \`slug\` is the current human-readable address. \`list_concepts\`, \`get_concept\`, \`get_concepts\`, compiled/query node rows, and agent handoffs return both. Use \`get_concept({uid})\` or \`get_concepts({uids:[...]})\` for exact continuity across renames; use slug for frontmatter relations, URLs, and all graph-operation inputs. Never treat a slug change as a new UID.

2026-08-25T05:39:11.496166Z ERROR codex_models_manager::manager: failed to renew cache TTL: missing field `supports_parallel_tool_calls` at line 97 column 5
codex
핵심적으로 ACP runtime capability는 **“앱이 이미 설치된 코딩 에이전트를 ACP로 실행하고, 볼트 범위와 쓰기 권한을 앱이 통제하는 실행 계층”**입니다. ACP 자체가 온톨로지 스키마나 의미 판단 엔진인 것은 아닙니다. [capability 정의](</Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/ontology/capabilities/acp-runtime.md:17>)

먼저 현재 상태의 중요한 불일치가 있습니다.

- 저장된 온톨로지 본문은 아직 “인앱 채팅은 현재 `claude-acp`만 가능하고 Codex는 외부 연결만 가능”하다고 명시합니다. [경계 정의](</Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/ontology/capabilities/acp-runtime.md:38>)
- 그러나 최신 결정과 구현은 Codex의 MCP 쓰기도 Atlas 서버의 `OATLAS_WRITE_CONSENT`로 막도록 바꾸었고, 현재 Rust의 `CHAT_ELIGIBLE`에는 `claude-acp`와 `codex-acp`가 모두 들어 있습니다. [결정 113](</Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/DECISIONS.md:122), [현재 실행기 경계](</Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/src-tauri/src/acp.rs:165)

따라서 아래는 “온톨로지에 선언된 경계”와 “최신 구현상의 경계”를 함께 반영한 설명입니다.

| 포함 | 제외 | 그렇게 나눈 이유 |
|---|---|---|
| ACP v1 실행기 탐지와 준비 상태 구분: `ready`, `cli-missing`, `node-missing`, `uvx-missing`, `binary-missing` | 단순한 설치됨/없음 판정 | 상태별로 사용자의 다음 조치가 다르기 때문 |
| 사용자가 이미 설치한 에이전트의 앱 내 실행 | Atlas가 모델·벤더·API 키를 제공하거나 에이전트를 자체 서비스로 운영하는 것 | 기존 에이전트의 구독 인증을 사용하고, Atlas는 실행기와 경계만 책임지기 때문 |
| 앱 전용 설정 격리와 자격증명 심볼릭 링크 | 사용자의 전역 설정을 그대로 상속하거나 비밀을 복사하는 것 | 전역 설정에 `allow all`이 있으면 앱의 권한 약속이 무력화되기 때문 |
| 현재 볼트에 연결된 MCP 서버 주입 | ACP가 MCP 서버를 대체하는 것 | ACP는 MCP 도구 위에 올라가는 실행 경로이며, 실제 온톨로지 읽기/쓰기는 MCP가 담당하기 때문 |
| 볼트 안 읽기의 자동 진행, 온톨로지 쓰기의 typed change review, `allow_once`/`reject_once` | 볼트 밖·불명확한 경로의 자동 허용, 의미 쓰기의 `allow_always` | 사람의 의미 결정권과 로컬 파일 경계를 보존하기 위해서입니다. [쓰기 검토 요소](</Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/ontology/elements/acp-ontology-write-review.md:12>) |
| 세션별 볼트 루트 고정, 경로 정규화, 프로세스 트리 종료, 지도 포커스/최단경로 동기화 | 화면이 `vaultRoot`를 다시 지정하거나 자연어 답변을 파싱해 지도를 움직이는 것 | 권한 경계가 화면 입력이나 모델의 문장에 의해 변하지 않도록 하기 위해서입니다. [네이티브 경계 구현](</Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/src-tauri/src/lib.rs:1081>) |
| `Agents` 목적지와 Home의 인셋 대화 패널 | ACP를 별도의 독립적인 온톨로지 Studio나 주 작업면으로 승격하는 것 | 제품의 주 작업면은 여전히 지도이고, ACP는 지도와 연결된 대화 표면이기 때문입니다. [초기 ACP 결정](</Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/DECISIONS.md:5799>) |

실행기 자격은 특히 엄격합니다.

- `isolated`는 “설정 디렉터리를 격리할 수 있다”는 뜻일 뿐, 쓰기 관문이 실제로 작동한다는 뜻은 아닙니다.
- Claude는 앱 전용 설정으로 자신의 권한 관문을 소유합니다.
- Codex는 `read-only`만으로는 Atlas MCP 쓰기를 막지 못했기 때문에, 최신 구현에서는 **Atlas MCP 서버가 직접 쓰기 동의를 묻는 방식**으로 보완했습니다. 서버가 `elicitation`을 사용할 수 없으면 fail-closed로 쓰기를 거부합니다. [서버 측 동의 관문](</Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/mcp/src/write-consent.mjs:14>)
- 아직 측정되지 않은 실행기는 탐지 목록에는 보일 수 있지만, 앱 내 대화 자격을 자동으로 얻지 못합니다.

마지막으로 ACP가 온톨로지 쓰기 자체를 소유하는 것도 아닙니다. ACP는 “쓰기 요청을 멈추고 사용자에게 보여 주는 입구”이고, 실제 typed change set·검토·Markdown frontmatter 적용은 별도의 `reviewed-ontology-writing` capability가 소유합니다. [검토 뒤 온톨로지 쓰기](</Users/stark/orca/workspaces/oh-my-ontology/main-2/docs/ontology/capabilities/reviewed-ontology-writing.md:15>) 따라서 이 경계의 목적은 한 문장으로 말하면 **“에이전트 실행의 편의는 ACP에 주되, 파일 경계와 의미의 최종 결정권은 Atlas와 사람에게 남기는 것”**입니다.
